without loss of generality. We introduce the feature vector for compatibility with linear function approximation. Nevertheless, we emphasize that our algorithm still allows general function approximation class by choosing
\lambda=0
. See Algorithm
4
for the complete algorithm.

We point out that Algorithm
4
reduces to Algorithm
2
if we choose
t_{0}=0
and let
F
be the temporal difference
\delta_{i_{0}}(t)
in Algorithm
2
. Besides, many other classical single agent policy evaluation algorithm, such as LSTD
(
Boyan, 1999
)
, are a special case of Algorithm
4
.

Algorithm 4

Generalized TD(
\lambda
)

1:

Input
:

\tau_{\kappa_{c}}

2:

Parameter
:
\lambda\in[0,1)
,
t_{0}\geq 0
, function
F:(\mathcal{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})^{t_{0}+2}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
.

Initialization
:
w(0):=0
,
\zeta(0):=\psi(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(0))
.

3:

for

t=t_{0},t_{0}+1,\cdots,K-1

do

4:

X(t):=(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(t-t_{0}),\cdots,z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(t+1))
.

5:

Update parameter
w(t+1)=w(t)+\alpha F(X(t),w(t))\zeta(t)
.

6:

Update eligibility vector
\zeta(t+1)=\lambda\zeta(t)+\psi(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(t+1))
.

7:

end

for

8:

Return

w(K)

C.3  Convergence result

To make the section self-contatined, we restate assumptions needed for localized stochastic approximation. We point out that all assumptions below can be satisfied by localized TD(
\lambda
) with linear function approximation under the assumptions in the main text.

Assumption C.1
.

\mathbb{P}
is aperiodic and irreducible.

Assumption
C.1
guarantees the existence and uniqueness of stationary distribution. In addition, Assumption
C.1
ensures that there exists
c^{\prime}>1
and
\rho^{\prime}\in(0,1)
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}(\pi_{t},\overline{\pi})\leq c^{\prime}\rho^{\prime t}.

(15)

We further define the stationary distribution of
\zeta(t)

(
Tsitsiklis and Van Roy, 1997
)
. Consider a stationary Markov process
\{z(t)\}
(
-\infty<t<\infty
), in which the state distribution at each time
t
is the stationary distribution. Let

\displaystyle\overline{\zeta}(t)=\sum_{k=-\infty}^{t}\lambda^{t-k}\psi(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(k)),

where
\{z(k)\}
is sampled from the stationary Markov process.
\overline{\zeta}(t)
is well-defined, and its distribution is invariant of
t
. Thus we can use the distribution of
\overline{\zeta}(t)
under stationary Markov process as the stationary distribution of
\zeta(t)
. We use
\overline{\mathbb{E}}[\cdot]
to represent the expected value of a formula, given that
\{z(t)\}
follows the stationary Markov process and
\zeta(t)
is sampled from the defined stationary distribution.

Let
G(\zeta(t),X,w)=\zeta(t)F(X,w)
and denote

\displaystyle\overline{G}(w):=\overline{\mathbb{E}}[\zeta(t)F(X,w)].

Assumption C.2
.

1.

There exists
L_{1}>1
such that

\displaystyle\left\lvert F(x,w_{1})-F(x,w_{2})\right\rvert\leq L_{1}\left\lVert w_{1}-w_{2}\right\rVert,\forall w_{1},w_{2},x

\displaystyle\left\lvert F(x,0)\right\rvert\leq L_{1},\forall x

2.

\overline{G}(w)
has a unique zero
w^{*}
. In addition, there exists
c_{0}>0
such that

\displaystyle(w-w^{*})^{\top}\overline{G}(w)\leq-c_{0}\|w-w^{*}\|^{2},\forall w\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

Point 1. ensures that our updating term is Lipschitz, while point 2. guarantees the existence of negative drift, which is crucial in Lyapunov analysis. Both assumptions are standard in non-linear stochastic approximation problems
(
Chen et al., 2022
)
.

Assumption C.3
.

\hat{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},w)
is
L_{2}
-Lipschitz with respect to
w
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\hat{C}(z_{\mathcal{N}_{i}^{\kappa}},w_{1})-\hat{C}(z_{\mathcal{N}_{i}^{\kappa}},w_{2})\right\rvert\leq L_{2}\|w_{1}-w_{2}\|

for all
z_{\mathcal{N}_{i}^{\kappa}}\in\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{N}_{i}^{\kappa}},w_{1},w_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Given the assumptions above, we can show a geometric mixing rate of
G(\zeta(t),X(t),w)
to
\overline{G}(w)
w.r.t.
t
. To be concrete, there exists
c_{g}(c^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\lambda,t_{0})>0
and
\rho_{g}(\rho^{\prime},\lambda)\in(0,1)
, such that for any
t\geq t_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbb{E}[G(\zeta(t),X(t),w)]-\overline{G}(w)\right\rVert\leq L_{1}(\|w\|+1)c_{g}(c^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\lambda,t_{0})[\rho_{g}(\rho^{\prime},\lambda)]^{t}.

(16)

This can be viewed as a generalized result of
(
Bertsekas and Tsitsiklis, 1996
, Lemma 6.7)
, and we defer the proof to Appendix
C.5.2
. In order to state conditions on stepsize, we
introduce the concept of mixing time of function
G
.

Definition C.1

(Mixing time of function
G
)
.

The mixing time of function
G
with precision
\delta
, for any
\delta>0
, is defined as

\displaystyle t_{\delta}^{\prime}:=\min\left\{t\geq 1\;\middle|\;\left\lVert\mathbb{E}[G(\zeta_{t},X(t),w)]-\bar{G}(w)\right\rVert\leq\delta L_{1}(\left\lVert w\right\rVert+1)\right\}.

Eq. (
15
) ensures that

\displaystyle t_{\delta}^{\prime}=O(\log(1/\delta))

for any
\delta>0
,
so
\lim_{\delta\rightarrow 0}\delta t_{\delta}^{\prime}=0
.

The performance of Algorithm
4
is related to its globalized version on state space
\mathcal{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}
. To illustrate this idea, we define the concept “sub-chain”.

Construct transition probability
\overline{\mathbb{P}}
on state space
\mathcal{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}
satisfying

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{P}}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime}|z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})=\sum_{z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}}\frac{\overline{\pi}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})}{\overline{\pi}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})}\mathbb{P}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime}|(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})).

(17)

Here

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime}|(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})=\sum_{z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime},z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime}|(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}).

Notice that
\overline{\pi}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})>0
due to Assumption
C.1
.

Definition C.2
.

Let
\mathcal{E}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}\times{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}
denote the edges with two end points in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}
. Let
\mu_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime}
denote the marginal initial state distribution of state space
\mathcal{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}
.

Then
Markov chain

{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}}=({\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}},\mathcal{E}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},\mathcal{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},\overline{\mathbb{P}},\tilde{r},\gamma,\mu^{\prime}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})
is called the
sub-chain
of Markov chain
(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{Z},\mathbb{P},\tilde{r},\gamma,\mu^{\prime})
with respect to agents
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}
.

We denote by
\tilde{C}:\mathcal{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
the sub-chain’s cost function.

The following concept is critical for the reduction of a localized algorithm to its globalized version.

Definition C.3
.

The reduction error is defined as

\displaystyle\epsilon_{red}:=\sup_{z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}\in\mathcal{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}}\left\lvert\hat{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},w^{*})-\tilde{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})\right\rvert.

(18)

The reduction error is dependent on the actual algorithm used, including update rule, choice of function approximation class.

We now present our main theorem of localized stochastic approximation. The proof sketch is given in subsection
C.4
, and the detailed proofs are deferred to section
C.5
.

Theorem C.1
.

Suppose Assumptions

C.1
,
C.2
and

C.3

are satisfied.
Choose stepsize
\alpha
satisfying
\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}\leq\min\left\{\frac{1}{4L_{1}^{\prime}},\frac{c_{0}}{114L_{1}^{\prime 2}}\right\}
, where
L_{1}^{\prime}=\frac{L_{1}}{1-\lambda}
.
Then we have for all
K\geq t_{\alpha}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{z}\left\lvert\hat{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},w(K))-C(z)\right\rvert\right]\leq

\displaystyle 3\left[L_{2}^{2}\left(c_{1}(1-c_{0}\alpha)^{K-t_{\alpha}^{\prime}}+c_{2}\frac{\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}}{c_{0}}\right)\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\epsilon_{red}^{2}+\left(\frac{\gamma^{\kappa_{c}-\kappa_{r}+1}}{1-\gamma}\right)^{2}\right].

Here
c_{1}=(\|w(0)\|+\|w(0)-w^{*}\|+1)^{2}
,

c_{2}=114L_{1}^{\prime 2}(\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2}
,

\epsilon_{red}
is defined in Definition
C.3
.

Notice that
t_{\delta}^{\prime}=O(\log\frac{1}{\delta})
. So
\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}\rightarrow 0
when
\alpha\rightarrow 0
. Thus the conditions for
\alpha
in Theorem
C.1
can be satisfied when
\alpha
is small enough.

C.4  Proof of Theorem
C.1

Key Idea: A Reduction Approach

The key to analyzing the localized stochastic approximation algorithm is to reduce it to a globalized policy evaluation algorithm on the sub-chain with respect to agents
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}
. To be specific, Algorithm
4
itself can be regarded as a globalized policy evaluation algorithm for the sub-chain
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}}
.

The most important part of the proof is that the sub-chain has the following properties:

1.

The “sub-chain” is an aperiodic and irreducible Markov chain.

2.

The local transition probabilities of agents in
N_{i_{0}}^{\kappa_{c}-1}
are the same for the sub-chain and the original chain. The local transition probabilities in the sub-chain do not need to be independent.

3.

The stationary distribution of sub-chain equals the marginal stationary distribution of
\mathcal{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}
in the original chain.

We formulate the properties as the three lemmas below. The proofs are deferred to Appendix
C.5.1
.

Lemma C.1
.

\overline{\mathbb{P}}
is aperiodic and irreducible.

Lemma C.2
.

We have for any agent
i\in N_{i_{0}}^{\kappa_{c}-1}
that

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{P}}_{i}(z_{i}^{\prime}|z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})=\mathbb{P}_{i}(z_{i}^{\prime}|z_{N_{i}}),\forall z_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{i},z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}\in\mathcal{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}.

(19)

Notice that
N_{i}\subseteq{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}
when
i\in N_{i_{0}}^{\kappa_{c}-1}
.

Lemma C.3
.

Consider the stationary Markov chain
(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{Z},\mathbb{P},\tilde{r},\gamma,\overline{\pi})
. That is, the initial state distribution

\mu^{\prime}=\overline{\pi}
is the stationary distribution.

Then for any
t\in\mathbb{N}
,
z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}
, we have

\displaystyle\Pr\left[z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(t+1)=z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime}|z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(t)=z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}\right]=\overline{\mathbb{P}}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime}|z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}).

Now we can discuss the proof of Theorem
C.1
. We can think that Algorithm
4
is executed on the local chain
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\mathcal{M}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}}
, with cost function denoted by
\tilde{C}
. Then
w^{*}
can be regarded as the fix point of parameter update in a globalized algorithm. In this regard, we can do the following error decomposition.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{z}\left\lvert\hat{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},w(t))-C(z)\right\rvert^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{z}\left(\left\lvert\hat{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},w(t))-\hat{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},w^{*})\right\rvert+\left\lvert\hat{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},w^{*})-\tilde{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})\right\rvert\right.\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\left.\left\lvert\tilde{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})-C(z)\right\rvert\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}

\displaystyle 3\left\{\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{z}\left\lvert\hat{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},w(t))-\hat{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},w^{*})\right\rvert^{2}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{z}\left\lvert\hat{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},w^{*})-\tilde{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})\right\rvert^{2}\right]\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{z}\left\lvert\tilde{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})-C(z)\right\rvert^{2}\right]\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3\left\{\underbrace{\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{z_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}\left\lvert\hat{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},w(t))-\hat{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},w^{*})\right\rvert^{2}\right]}_{(a)}+\underbrace{\sup_{z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}}\left\lvert\hat{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},w^{*})-\tilde{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})\right\rvert^{2}}_{(b)}\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\underbrace{\sup_{z}\left\lvert\tilde{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})-C(z)\right\rvert^{2}}_{(c)}\right\}.

(20)

Here
(i)
is by Cauchy-Schwarz inequality.

To interpret the error terms,

(a)
is related to the convergence of
w(t)
to the fix point
w^{*}
, which can be viewed as a globalized non-linear stochastic approximation problem
(
Chen et al., 2022
)
.

(b)
is the inherent property of the globalized algorithm and the Markov chain, which is defined as
\epsilon_{red}
in Definition
C.3
. We point out that for some algorithms, such as TD(
\lambda
) with linear function approximation,
\epsilon_{red}
can be bounded by the function approximation error.

(c)
is the difference of the cost function in the sub-chain and in the original chain, which originates from the use of a localized algorithm and decays exponentially with
\kappa_{c}
.

Bounding
(a)

This can be done in two steps. The first step is to analyze the convergence rate of
w(t)
to the stationary point
w^{*}
, which can be viewed as a stochastic approximation problem. Thus we can adopt Lyapunov approach to bound the convergence rate, which is a standard method in stochastic approximation
(
Srikant and Ying, 2019
;
Chen et al., 2022
)
.

The second step is to combine the Lipschitz assumption of
\hat{C}
(Assumption
C.3
) with convergence result of
w(t)
and derive the bound of
(a)
.

With the proof idea above, we proceed to bound the convergence rate of
w(t)
. In order to utilize the mixing of function
G(\zeta(t),X(t),w(t))
, we take expectation conditioned on
X(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime})
,
\zeta(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime})
and
w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime})
. For convenience of notation, we use
\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}[\cdot]=\mathbb{E}[\cdot|X(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime}),\zeta(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime}),w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime})]
.

For any
t\geq t_{\alpha}^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert w(t+1)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[(w(t)-w^{*})^{\top}(w(t+1)-w(t))\right]+\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[(w(t+1)-w(t))^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\alpha\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[(w(t)-w^{*})^{\top}\overline{G}(w(t)\right]}_{(a_{1})}

\displaystyle+2\alpha\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[(w(t)-w^{*})^{\top}(G(\zeta(t),X(t),w(t))-\overline{G}(w(t))\right]}_{(a_{2})}

\displaystyle+\alpha^{2}\underbrace{\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert G(\zeta(t),X(t),w(t))\right\rVert^{2}\right]}_{(a_{3})}.

(21)

Term
(a_{1})
corresponds to the negative drift, and Assumption
C.2
indicates that

\displaystyle(a_{1})\leq-c_{0}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}[\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}],

Before we bound
(a_{2})
and
(a_{3})
, we show that function
G
is Lipschitz. See Appendix
C.5.3
for the proof.

Lemma C.4
.

Let
L_{1}^{\prime}=\frac{L_{1}}{1-\lambda}
. Then we have for all
t,x
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert G(\zeta(t),x,w_{1})-G(\zeta(t),x,w_{2})\right\rVert\leq L_{1}^{\prime}\left\lVert w_{1}-w_{2}\right\rVert,\forall w_{1},w_{2}

\displaystyle\left\lVert G(\zeta(t),x,0)\right\rVert\leq L_{1}^{\prime}.

Besides, Lemma
C.4
also implies for any
t,x,w,w_{1},w_{2}
that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\overline{G}(w_{1})-\overline{G}(w_{2})\right\rVert\leq\overline{\mathbb{E}}\left[\left\lVert G(\zeta(t),X(t),w_{1})-G(\zeta(t),X(t),w_{2})\right\rVert\right]\leq L_{1}^{\prime}\left\lVert w_{1}-w_{2}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\left\lVert G(\zeta(t),x,w)\right\rVert\leq\left\lVert G(\zeta(t),x,w)-G(\zeta(t),x,0)\right\rVert+\left\lVert G(\zeta(t),x,0)\right\rVert\leq L_{1}^{\prime}(\left\lVert w\right\rVert+1)

\displaystyle\left\lVert\overline{G}(w)\right\rVert\leq\overline{\mathbb{E}}[\left\lVert G(\zeta(t),X(t),w]\right\rVert\leq L_{1}^{\prime}(\left\lVert w\right\rVert+1).

We apply Lemma
C.4
and bound term
(a_{3})
as

\displaystyle(a_{3})=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert G(\zeta(t),X(t),w(t))\right\rVert^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle L_{1}^{\prime 2}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[(\left\lVert w(t)\right\rVert+1)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle L_{1}^{\prime 2}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[(\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert+\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2L_{1}^{\prime 2}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}+(\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2}\right].

Finally we bound
(a_{2})
. Before that, we need to control the difference of
w(t_{1})
and
w(t_{2})
for any
t_{1},t_{2}
under certain conditions of stepsize. This can be formulated as the lemma below, with proof deferred to Appendix
C.5.4
.

Lemma C.5
.

For any
t_{1}>t_{2}\geq 0
, if
\alpha(t_{1}-t_{2})\leq\frac{1}{4L_{1}^{\prime}}
, then we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert w(t_{1})-w(t_{2})\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq 2L_{1}^{\prime}\alpha(t_{1}-t_{2})(\left\lVert w(t_{2})\right\rVert+1)

\displaystyle\leq 4L_{1}^{\prime}\alpha(t_{1}-t_{2})(\left\lVert w(t_{1})\right\rVert+1).

With Lemma
C.5
, we are able to control
(a_{2})
. The proof is given in Appendix
C.5.5
.

Lemma C.6
.

Suppose
\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}\leq\frac{1}{4L_{1}^{\prime}}
. Then the following inequality holds for all
t\geq t_{\alpha}^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle(a_{2})\leq 56L_{1}^{\prime 2}\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}+(\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2}\right].

Now we apply the upper bounds for
(a_{1})
,
(a_{2})
and
(a_{3})
to Eq. (
21
), and we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert w(t+1)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\alpha(a_{1})+2\alpha(a_{2})+\alpha^{2}(a_{3})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-2c_{0}\alpha\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}[\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}]

\displaystyle+112L_{1}^{\prime 2}\alpha^{2}t_{\alpha}^{\prime}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}+(\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+2L_{1}^{\prime 2}\alpha^{2}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}+(\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-2c_{0}\alpha\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}[\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}]

\displaystyle+114L_{1}^{\prime 2}\alpha^{2}t_{\alpha}^{\prime}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}+(\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2}\right].

Rearranging the terms, we derive that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert w(t+1)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(1-2c_{0}\alpha+114L_{1}^{\prime 2}\alpha^{2}t_{\alpha}^{\prime}\right)\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\right]+114L_{1}^{\prime 2}\alpha^{2}t_{\alpha}^{\prime}(\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1-c_{0}\alpha)\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\right]+114L_{1}^{\prime 2}\alpha^{2}t_{\alpha}^{\prime}(\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2},

where the last inequality is due to
\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}\leq\frac{c_{0}}{114L_{1}^{\prime 2}}
. Taking total expectation on both sides, we get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert w(t+1)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\right]\leq(1-c_{0}\alpha)\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\right]+114L_{1}^{\prime 2}\alpha^{2}t_{\alpha}^{\prime}(\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2}.

Repeatedly use the above inequality starting from
t_{\alpha}^{\prime}
, and we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\right]\leq

\displaystyle(1-c_{0}\alpha)^{t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert w(t_{\alpha}^{\prime})-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+114L_{1}^{\prime 2}\alpha^{2}t_{\alpha}^{\prime}(\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime}-1}(1-c_{0}\alpha)^{k}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1-c_{0}\alpha)^{t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert w(t_{\alpha}^{\prime})-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\right]+\frac{114L_{1}^{\prime 2}\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}(\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2}}{c_{0}}.

We can bound
\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert w(t_{\alpha}^{\prime})-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\right]
by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert w(t_{\alpha}^{\prime})-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\right]\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left\lVert w(t_{\alpha}^{\prime})-w(0)\right\rVert+\left\lVert w(0)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left\lVert w(0)\right\rVert+\left\lVert w(0)-w^{*}\right\rVert+1\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle c_{1},

where
(i)
is by Lemma
C.5
, with
t_{1}=t_{\alpha}^{\prime}
and
t_{2}=0
, and by
\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}\leq\frac{1}{4L_{1}^{\prime}}
.

Noticing that
c_{2}=114L_{1}^{\prime 2}(\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2}
, we substitute
t
with
K
and get

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lVert w(K)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\right]\leq c_{1}(1-c_{0}\alpha)^{K-t_{\alpha}^{\prime}}+c_{2}\frac{\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}}{c_{0}},\;\forall K\geq t_{\alpha}^{\prime}.

(22)

For the second step, we combine Assumption
C.3
with Eq. (
22
) and get the following result, the proof of which is in Appendix
C.5.6
.

Lemma C.7
.

For
K>t_{\alpha}^{\prime}
, we have

\displaystyle(a)\leq L_{2}^{2}\left(c_{1}(1-\alpha c_{0})^{K-t_{\alpha}^{\prime}}+c_{2}\frac{\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}}{c_{0}}\right).

Bounding
(b)

We have
(b)=\epsilon_{red}^{2}
by Definition
C.3
.

Bounding
(c)

This can be derived by the exponential decay property of the cost function. See Appendix
C.5.7
for the proof.

Lemma C.8
.

The cost function of the sub-chain and the original chain differs by

\displaystyle\sup_{z}\left\lvert\tilde{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})-C(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\gamma^{\kappa_{c}-\kappa_{r}+1}.

Thus
(c)\leq\left(\frac{\gamma^{\kappa_{c}-\kappa_{r}+1}}{1-\gamma}\right)^{2}
.

Eventually. we combine bounds for
(a)
,
(b)
,
(c)
(Lemma
C.7
, Definition
C.3
and Lemma
C.8
) and plug into Eq. (
20
). Then we complete the proof.

C.5  Proofs of Technical Lemmas in Appendix
C

C.5.1  Properties of Sub-Chain

We give the proofs of Lemma
C.1
,
C.2
,
C.3
.

Proof of Lemma
C.1
.

For any Markov chain with transition probability
\Gamma
on some state space
\mathcal{X}
, we write
\Gamma^{k}(x^{\prime}|x)=\Pr[x(t+k)=x^{\prime}|x(t)=x
, for any
t\in\mathbb{N}
,
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}
.

For any

z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}
, we randomly pick
z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Z}_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}
and let
z=(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})
,

z^{\prime}=(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime},z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime})
. Since
\mathbb{P}
is irreducible, there exists
i_{0}>0
, such that

\mathbb{P}^{k}(z^{\prime}|z)>0
, so
\mathbb{P}^{k}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime}|z)>0
.
\mathbb{P}^{k}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime}|z)
represents the marginal probability of
z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime}
given previous state
z
. By the interpretation of
\overline{\mathbb{P}}
given in Lemma
C.3
,

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{P}}^{k}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime}|z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\hat{z}_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}\in\mathcal{Z}_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}}\frac{\overline{\pi}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},\hat{z}_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})}{\overline{\pi}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})}{\mathbb{P}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{k}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime}|z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},\hat{z}_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\overline{\pi}(z)}{\overline{\pi}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})}{\mathbb{P}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{k}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime}|z)

\displaystyle>0.

Therefore,
\overline{\mathbb{P}}
is irreducible.

To show that
\overline{\mathbb{P}}
is aperiodic, we assume that
\overline{\mathbb{P}}
has period
T\geq 2
. Then for any

i_{0}
not divisible by
T
and any
z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}
,
\overline{\mathbb{P}}^{k}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}|z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})=0
. For any

z\in\mathcal{Z}
, since for
i_{0}
not divisible by
T
,

\displaystyle 0=\overline{\mathbb{P}}^{k}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}|z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})=\sum_{\hat{z}_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}\in\mathcal{Z}_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}}\frac{\overline{\pi}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},\hat{z}_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})}{\pi_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{*}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})}{\mathbb{P}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{k}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}|(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},\hat{z}_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})),

we have
{\mathbb{P}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{k}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}|(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},\hat{z}_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}))=0
for any

\hat{z}_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}\in\mathcal{Z}_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}
. In particular,

\overline{\mathbb{P}}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{k}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}|z)=0
and thus
\overline{\mathbb{P}}^{k}(z|z)=0
. This implies that the
period of state
z
is at least
T\geq 2
, which contradicts the assumption that
\mathbb{P}
is aperiodic. Hence

\overline{\mathbb{P}}
is aperiodic.

In conclusion,
\overline{\mathbb{P}}
is irreducible and aperiodic.
∎

Proof of Lemma
C.2
.

For simplicity, let
I={\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}/\{i\}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{P}}_{i}(z_{i}^{\prime}|z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})=

\displaystyle\sum_{z_{I}^{\prime}}\overline{\mathbb{P}}(z_{i}^{\prime},z_{I}^{\prime}|z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{z_{I}^{\prime}}\sum_{z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}}\frac{\overline{\pi}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})}{\overline{\pi}_{\kappa_{c}}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})}\mathbb{P}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime}|(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}}\frac{\overline{\pi}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})}{\overline{\pi}_{\kappa_{c}}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})}\sum_{z_{I}^{\prime}}\mathbb{P}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime}|(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}))

\displaystyle\mathop{=}\limits^{(i)}

\displaystyle\sum_{z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}}\frac{\overline{\pi}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})}{\overline{\pi}_{\kappa_{c}}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})}\mathbb{P}_{i}(z_{i}^{\prime}|z_{N_{i}})

\displaystyle\mathop{=}\limits^{(ii)}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{i}(z_{i}^{\prime}|z_{N_{i}})\sum_{z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}}\frac{\overline{\pi}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})}{\overline{\pi}_{\kappa_{c}}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{i}(z_{i}^{\prime}|z_{N_{i}}).

Here
(i)
uses the fact that the local transition probability
\mathbb{P}_{i}
only depends on the states of agents in
N_{i}
, and
(ii)
is because
N_{i}\cap-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}=\varnothing
.
∎

Proof of Lemma
C.3
.

Since we consider the stationary Markov chain, the state distribution at any time
t
is equal to the stationary state distribution
\overline{\pi}
, so we have

\displaystyle\Pr\left[z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(t+1)=z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime}|z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(t)=z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\Pr\left[z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(t+1)=z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime},z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(t)=z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}\right]}{\Pr\left[z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(t)=z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}}\Pr\left[z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(t+1)=z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime},z(t)=(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})\right]}{\overline{\pi}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}}\overline{\pi}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})\mathbb{P}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime}|(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},z_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}))}{\overline{\pi}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\overline{\mathbb{P}}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\prime}|z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}).

∎

C.5.2  Geometric mixing of the function
G

We prove the geometric mixing property of function
G
(cf. Eq. (
16
)), which can be formalized as the lemma below:

Lemma C.9
.

There exists
c_{g}(c^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\lambda,t_{0})>0
and
\rho_{g}(\rho^{\prime},\lambda)\in(0,1)\in(0,1)
, such that for any
t\geq t_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbb{E}[G(\zeta(t),X(t),w)]-\overline{G}(w)\right\rVert\leq L_{1}(\|w\|+1)c_{g}(c^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\lambda,t_{0})[\rho_{g}(\rho^{\prime},\lambda)]^{t}.

To prove Lemma
C.9
, we need some auxiliary results. Let
Y^{t_{1}}(t)=(z(t-t_{1}),\cdots,z(t+1))\in\mathcal{Y}^{t_{1}}=\mathcal{Z}^{t_{1}+2}
. Denote by
\pi_{Y,t_{1},t}\in\Delta(\mathcal{Y}^{t_{1}})
the distribution of
Y^{t_{1}}(t)
and by
\overline{\pi}_{Y,t_{1}}\in\Delta(\mathcal{Y}^{t_{1}})
the corresponding stationary distribution.

\pi_{Y,t_{1},t}
and
\overline{\pi}_{Y,t_{1}}
can be computed by

\displaystyle\pi_{Y,t_{1},t}(z_{t-t_{1}},\cdots,z_{t+1})

\displaystyle=\pi_{t-t_{1}}(z_{t-t_{1}})\prod_{i=t-t_{1}}^{t}\mathbb{P}(z_{i+1}|z_{i})

(23)

\displaystyle\overline{\pi}_{Y}(z_{0},\cdots,z_{t_{1}+2})

\displaystyle=\overline{\pi}(z_{0})\prod_{i=0}^{t+1}\mathbb{P}(z_{i+1}|z_{i}).

(24)

The following lemma states the convergence rate of
\pi_{Y,t_{1},t}
.

Lemma C.10
.

For any
t\geq t_{1}
,
\mathrm{TV}(\pi_{Y,t_{1},t},\overline{\pi}_{Y,t_{1}})\leq c^{\prime}\rho^{\prime t-t_{1}}
.

Proof of Lemma
C.10
.

We have by Eqs. (
23
) and (
24
)

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}(\pi_{Y,t_{1},t},\overline{\pi}_{Y,t_{1}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{z_{t-t_{1}},\cdots,z_{t+1}}\left\lvert\pi_{Y,t_{1},t}(z_{t-t_{1}},\cdots,z_{t+1})-\overline{\pi}_{Y}(z_{t-t_{1}},\cdots,z_{t+1})\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{z_{t-t_{1}},\cdots,z_{t+1}}\left\lvert\pi_{t-t_{1}}(z_{t-t_{1}})-\overline{\pi}(z_{t-t_{1}})\right\rvert\prod_{i=t-t_{1}}^{t}\left[\mathbb{P}(z_{i+1}|z_{i})\right]|

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{z_{t-t_{1}}}\left\lvert\pi_{t-t_{1}}(z_{t-t_{1}})-\overline{\pi}(z_{t-t_{1}})\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\mathrm{TV}(\pi_{t-t_{1}},\overline{\pi})

\displaystyle\leq c^{\prime}\rho^{\prime t-t_{1}}.

∎

Lemma C.11
.

For
t\geq m
, any
w
and any
X(t)=(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(t-t_{0}),\cdots,z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(t+1))
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathbb{E}[\psi(z(t-m))F(X(t),w)]-\overline{\mathbb{E}}[\psi(z(t-m))F(X(t),w)]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2L_{1}(\|w\|+1)c^{\prime}\rho^{\prime t-\max\{m,t_{0}\}}.

Proof of Lemma
C.11
.

Let set
\mathcal{S}^{t_{1}}=\{(z(t-t_{1}),\cdots,z(t+1))\in\mathcal{Y}^{t_{1}}\mid(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(t-t_{1}),\cdots,z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(t+1)=X(t))\}
, for any
t_{1}\in\mathbb{N}
.

When
m\geq t_{0}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbb{E}[\psi(z(t-m))F(X(t),w)]-\overline{\mathbb{E}}[\psi(z(t-m))F(X(t),w)]\right\rVert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\lVert\sum_{Y^{m}\in\mathcal{S}^{m}}\psi(z(t-m))F(X(t),w)(\pi_{Y,m,t}(Y^{m})-\overline{\pi}_{Y,m}(Y^{m}))\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{Y^{m}\in\mathcal{S}^{m}}\left\lVert\psi(z(t-m))\right\rVert\left\lvert F(X(t),w)\right\rvert\left\lvert\pi_{Y,m,t}(Y^{m})-\overline{\pi}_{Y,m}(Y^{m}))\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle L_{1}(\left\lVert w\right\rVert+1)\sum_{Y^{m}\in\mathcal{S}^{m}}\left\lvert\pi_{Y,m,t}(Y^{m})-\overline{\pi}_{Y,m}(Y^{m}))\right\rvert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2L_{1}(\left\lVert w\right\rVert+1)\mathrm{TV}(\pi_{Y,m,t},\overline{\pi}_{Y,m})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2L_{1}(\left\lVert w\right\rVert+1)c^{\prime}\rho^{\prime t-m}.

Here the last inequality is due to Lemma
C.10
.

We can similarly prove the case for
m<t_{0}
. In that case,
z(t-m)
is included in
(z(t-t_{0}),\cdots,z(t+1)
, so we just need to replace
m
with
t_{0}
in the proof above.
∎

Now we can bound the convergence rate of function
G
.

Proof of Lemma
C.9
.

We have by definition of function
G
and
\overline{G}
that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbb{E}[G(\zeta(t),X(t),w)]-\overline{G}(w)\right\rVert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\lVert\sum_{m=0}^{t}\lambda^{m}\mathbb{E}[\psi(z(t-m))F(X(t),w)]-\sum_{m=0}^{\infty}\lambda^{m}\overline{\mathbb{E}}[\psi(z(t-m))F(X(t),w)]\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{t}\lambda^{m}\left\lVert\mathbb{E}[\psi(z(t-m))F(X(t),w)]-\overline{\mathbb{E}}[\psi(z(t-m))F(X(t),w)]\right\rVert

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sum_{m=t+1}^{\infty}\lambda^{m}\left\lVert\overline{\mathbb{E}}[\psi(z(t-m))F(X(t),w)\right\rVert.

Notice that Assumption
C.2
indicates that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\overline{\mathbb{E}}[\psi(z(t-m))F(X(t),w)]\right\rVert\leq L_{1}(\|w\|+1),

and Lemma
C.11
implies that

\displaystyle\left\lVert\mathbb{E}[\psi(z(t-m))F(X(t),w)]-\overline{\mathbb{E}}[\psi(z(t-m))F(X(t),w)]\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2L_{1}(\|w\|+1)c^{\prime}\rho^{\prime t-\max\{t_{0},m\}}.

Plug in the two bounds back, and we have

\displaystyle\|\mathbb{E}[G(\zeta(t),X(t),w)]-\overline{G}(w)\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{m=0}^{t_{0}-1}\lambda^{m}2L_{1}(\|w\|+1)c^{\prime}\rho^{\prime t-t_{0}}+\sum_{m=t_{0}}^{t}\lambda^{m}2L_{1}(\|w\|+1)c^{\prime}\rho^{\prime t-m}+\sum_{m=t+1}^{\infty}\lambda^{m}L_{1}(\|w\|+1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L_{1}(\|w\|+1)\left[2c^{\prime}\underbrace{\sum_{m=0}^{t_{0}-1}\lambda^{m}\rho^{\prime t-t_{0}}}_{(a)}+2c^{\prime}\underbrace{\sum_{m=t_{0}}^{t}\lambda^{m}\rho^{\prime t-m}}_{(b)}+\underbrace{\sum_{m=t+1}^{\infty}\lambda^{m}}\right]_{(c)}.

(25)

where we use Assumption
C.2
as well as Lemma
C.11
.
Obviously,
(a)=O(\rho^{\prime t})
and
(c)=O(\lambda^{t})
.
For
(b)
, there are three cases:

•

\lambda<\rho^{\prime}
, then
(b)=O(\rho^{\prime t})
.

•

\lambda=\rho^{\prime}
, then
(b)=(t-t_{0}+1)\rho^{\prime t}
, which also decays exponentially fast with
t
, with any decay rate less than
\rho^{\prime}
.

•

\lambda>\rho^{\prime}
, then
(b)=\sum_{m^{\prime}=0}^{t-t_{0}}\lambda^{t-m^{\prime}}\rho^{\prime m}=O(\lambda^{t})
.

In conclusion,
(a)
,
(b)
, and
(c)
all decays exponentiallly fast with
t
, with decay rates depending only on
\lambda
or
\rho^{\prime}
. Let
\rho^{\prime}(\rho^{\prime},\lambda)
be the maximum value among the three decay rates, then there exists some
c_{g}(c^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\lambda,t_{0})>0
, such that

\displaystyle 2c^{\prime}\sum_{m=0}^{t_{0}-1}\lambda^{m}\rho^{\prime t-t_{0}}+2c^{\prime}\sum_{m=t_{0}}^{t}\lambda^{m}\rho^{\prime t-m}+\sum_{m=t+1}^{\infty}\lambda^{m}\leq c_{g}(c^{\prime},\rho^{\prime},\lambda,t_{0})[\rho_{g}(\rho^{\prime},\lambda)]^{t}.

Plug into Eq. (
25
), and we complete the proof.
∎

C.5.3  Proof of Lemma
C.4

The key is to notice that

\displaystyle\|\zeta(t)\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{t}\lambda^{t-k}\|\psi(z(k))\|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{t}\lambda^{t-k}

\displaystyle<\frac{1}{1-\lambda}.

Then by Assumption
C.2
, we get

\displaystyle\left\lVert G(\zeta(t),x,w_{1})-G(\zeta(t),x,w_{2})\right\rVert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\lVert\zeta(t)[F(x,w_{1})-F(x,w_{2})]\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\lVert\zeta(t)\right\rVert\left\lvert F(x,w_{1})-F(x,w_{2})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\lambda}\cdot L_{1}\left\lVert w_{1}-w_{2}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L_{1}^{\prime}\left\lVert w_{1}-w_{2}\right\rVert.

Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert G(\zeta(t),x,0)\right\rVert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\lVert\zeta(t)F(x,0)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\lVert\zeta(t)\right\rVert\left\lvert F(x,0)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{L_{1}}{1-\lambda}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle L_{1}^{\prime}.

C.5.4  Proof of Lemma
C.5

By Lemma
C.4
, we have for ant
t
that

\displaystyle\left\lVert w(t)\right\rVert-\left\lVert w(t-1)\right\rVert\leq

\displaystyle\left\lVert w(t)-w(t-1)\right\rVert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\alpha\left\lVert G(\zeta_{t-1},X(t-1),w(t-1)\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\alpha L_{1}^{\prime}(\left\lVert w(t-1)\right\rVert+1).

So
\left\lVert w(t)\right\rVert+1\leq(1+\alpha L_{1}^{\prime})(\left\lVert w(t-1)\right\rVert+1)
. Then we have for any
t\geq t_{2}
,

\displaystyle\left\lVert w(t)\right\rVert+1\leq(1+\alpha L_{1}^{\prime})^{t-t_{2}}(\left\lVert w(t_{2})\right\rVert+1).

Therefore, we get

\displaystyle\left\lVert w(t_{1})-w(t_{2})\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{2}}^{t_{1}-1}\left\lVert w(t^{\prime}+1)-w(t^{\prime})\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{2}}^{t_{1}-1}\alpha L_{1}^{\prime}(\left\lVert w(t^{\prime})\right\rVert+1)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{t^{\prime}=t_{2}}^{t_{1}-1}\alpha L_{1}^{\prime}(1+\alpha L_{1}^{\prime})^{t^{\prime}-t_{2}}(\left\lVert w(t_{2})\right\rVert+1)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left[(1+\alpha L_{1}^{\prime})^{t_{1}-t_{2}}-1\right](\left\lVert w(t_{2})\right\rVert+1).

Notice that
(1+x)^{p}\leq 1+2px
for any
p>0
and
x\in[0,\frac{1}{2p}]
.
So for
\alpha(t_{1}-t_{2})\leq\frac{1}{2L_{1}^{\prime}}
, we have

\displaystyle(1+\alpha L_{1}^{\prime})^{t_{1}-t_{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 1+2L_{1}^{\prime}\alpha(t_{1}-t_{2}).

Therefore, we have
\left\lVert w(t_{1})-w(t_{2})\right\rVert\leq 2L_{1}^{\prime}\alpha(t_{1}-t_{2})(\left\lVert w(t_{2})\right\rVert+1)
.

Since
\alpha(t_{1}-t_{2})\leq\frac{1}{4L_{1}^{\prime}}
, we further have

\displaystyle\left\lVert w(t_{1})-w(t_{2})\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2L_{1}^{\prime}\alpha(t_{1}-t_{2})(\left\lVert w(t_{2})\right\rVert+1)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2L_{1}^{\prime}\alpha(t_{1}-t_{2})(\left\lVert w(t_{1})-w(t_{2})\right\rVert+\left\lVert w(t_{1})\right\rVert+1)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\lVert w(t_{1})-w(t_{2})\right\rVert+2L_{1}^{\prime}\alpha(t_{1}-t_{2})(\left\lVert w(t_{1})\right\rVert+1).

Rearrange the terms, and we have
\left\lVert w(t_{1})-w(t_{2})\right\rVert\leq 4L_{1}^{\prime}\alpha(t_{1}-t_{2})(\left\lVert w(t_{1})\right\rVert+1)
.

C.5.5  Proof of Lemma
C.6

We can decompose
(a_{2})
as

\displaystyle(a_{2})=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[(w(t)-w^{*})^{\top}\left(G(\zeta(t),X(t),w(t))-\overline{G}(w(t))\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[(w(t)-w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime})^{\top}\left(G(\zeta(t),X(t),w(t))-\overline{G}(w(t))\right)\right]

(
b_{1}
)

\displaystyle+(w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime})-w^{*})^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[G(\zeta(t),X(t),w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime}))-\overline{G}(w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime}))\right]

(
b_{2}
)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[(w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime})-w^{*})^{\top}\left(G(\zeta(t),X(t),w(t))-G(\zeta(t),X(t),w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime}))\right)\right]

(
b_{3}
)

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[(w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime})-w^{*})^{\top}\left(\overline{G}(w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime}))-\overline{G}(w(t))\right)\right].

(
b_{4}
)

For term
(b_{1})
, we apply Lemma
C.5
with
t_{1}=t
and
t_{2}=t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime}
. Thus we get

\displaystyle(b_{1})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert w(t)-w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime})\right\rVert\left\lVert G(\zeta(t),X(t),w(t)-\overline{G}(w(t)\right\rVert\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert w(t)-w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime})\right\rVert\left(\left\lVert G(\zeta(t),X(t),w(t)\right\rVert+\left\lVert\overline{G}(w(t)\right\rVert\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq 4L_{1}^{\prime}\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[(\left\lVert w(t)\right\rVert+1)\left(\left\lVert G(\zeta(t),X(t),w(t)\right\rVert+\left\lVert\overline{G}(w(t)\right\rVert\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq 8L_{1}^{\prime 2}\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[(\left\lVert w(t)\right\rVert+1)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 8L_{1}^{\prime 2}\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[(\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert+\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq 16L_{1}^{\prime 2}\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}+(\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=16L_{1}^{\prime 2}\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\right]+(\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2}\right).

(26)

Now we bound
(b_{2})
. We have by Definition
C.1
that

\displaystyle(b_{2})

\displaystyle\leq\left\lVert w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime})-w^{*}\right\rVert\left\lVert\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[(G(\zeta(t),X(t),w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime}))-\overline{G}(w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime})\right]\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq L_{1}\alpha(\left\lVert w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime})\right\rVert+1)\left\lVert w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime})-w^{*}\right\rVert

\displaystyle=L_{1}\alpha\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[(\left\lVert w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime})\right\rVert+1)\left\lVert w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime})-w^{*}\right\rVert\right].

We further bound
(b_{2})
. By Lemma
C.5
and the fact that
\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}\leq\frac{1}{4L_{1}^{\prime}}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert w(t)-w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime})\right\rVert\leq 4L_{1}^{\prime}\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}(\left\lVert w(t)\right\rVert+1)\leq\left\lVert w(t)\right\rVert+1.

(27)

Thus we have

\displaystyle(\left\lVert w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime})\right\rVert+1)\left\lVert w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime})-w^{*}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\left\lVert w(t)-w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime})\right\rVert+\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert+\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)(\left\lVert w(t)-w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime})\right\rVert+\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\left\lVert w(t)\right\rVert+\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert+\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+2)(\left\lVert w(t)\right\rVert+\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert+1)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(2\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert+2\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+2)(2\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert+\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4(\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert+\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8\left[\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}+(\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2}\right].

So we can bound
(b_{2})
as

\displaystyle(b_{2})\leq 8L_{1}\alpha\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}+(\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2}\right].

(28)

Finally we bound the sum of
(b_{3})
and
(b_{4})
. We apply Lemma
C.4
and get

\displaystyle(b_{3})+(b_{4})\leq

\displaystyle 2L_{1}^{\prime}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime})-w^{*}\right\rVert\left\lVert w(t)-w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime})\right\rVert\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8L_{1}^{\prime 2}\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime})-w^{*}\right\rVert(\left\lVert w(t)\right\rVert+1)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8L_{1}^{\prime 2}\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[(\left\lVert w(t)-w(t-t_{\alpha}^{\prime})\right\rVert+\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert)(\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert+\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8L_{1}^{\prime 2}\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[(\left\lVert w(t)\right\rVert+\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert+1)(\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert+\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 8L_{1}^{\prime 2}\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[(2\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert+\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)(\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert+\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16L_{1}^{\prime 2}\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[(\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert+\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 32L_{1}^{\prime 2}\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}+(\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2}\right].

(29)

Now we combine the bounds for
(b_{1}),(b_{2}),(b_{3}),(b_{4})
(Eq. (
26
), (
28
), (
29
)) and get

\displaystyle(a_{2})=

\displaystyle(b_{1})+(b_{2})+(b_{3})+(b_{4})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 16L_{1}^{\prime 2}\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\right]+(\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2}\right)

\displaystyle+8L_{1}\alpha\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}+(\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle+32L_{1}^{\prime 2}\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}+(\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 56L_{1}^{\prime 2}\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\left[\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}+(\left\lVert w^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2}\right],

where the last inequality is derived by
1<L_{1}<L_{1}^{\prime}
(cf. Assumption
C.2
) and
t_{\alpha}^{\prime}\geq 1
.

C.5.6  Proof of Lemma
C.7

By Assumption
C.3
, we have for any
t

\displaystyle\left\lvert\hat{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},w(t))-\hat{C}(z,w^{*})\right\rvert\leq

\displaystyle L_{2}\left\lVert w(t)-w^{*}\right\rVert.

So we get for
K\geq t_{\alpha}^{\prime}
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{z}\left\lvert\hat{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},w(K))-\hat{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},w^{*})\right\rvert^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[L_{2}^{2}\left\lVert w(K)-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq L_{2}^{2}\left(c_{1}(1-\alpha c_{0})^{K-t_{\alpha}^{\prime}}+c_{2}\frac{\alpha t_{\alpha}^{\prime}}{c_{0}}\right).

C.5.7  Proof of Lemma
C.8

Denote by
\pi_{\kappa_{r},t}
the marginal distribution of
\mathcal{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{r}}}}
at time
t
in the original Markov chain (state space
\mathcal{Z}
). Let
\tilde{\pi}_{\kappa_{r},t}
denote the marginal state distribution at time
t
in the sub-chain (state space
\mathcal{Z}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}
.)

Due to the local dependence of transition probability (cf. Eq. (
11
)),
\pi_{\kappa_{r},t}
is only dependent on the initial states of agents in
N_{i_{0}}^{\kappa_{r}+t}
, which is equal to
\tilde{\pi}_{t}^{\kappa_{r}}
when
t\leq\kappa_{c}-\kappa_{r}
. Therefore we have for any
z\in\mathcal{Z}

\displaystyle\left\lvert\tilde{C}(z_{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}})-C(z)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\gamma^{t}\tilde{r}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{r}}}}(t))|z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(0)=z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}\right]-\mathbb{E}\left[\gamma^{t}\tilde{r}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{r}}}}(t))|z(0)=z\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\left\lvert\gamma^{t}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{r}}}}\sim\tilde{\pi}_{\kappa_{r},t}}\tilde{r}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{r}}}})-\gamma^{t}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{r}}}}\sim\pi_{\kappa_{r},t}}\tilde{r}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{r}}}})\right\rvert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{t=\kappa_{c}-\kappa_{r}+1}^{\infty}\left\lvert\gamma^{t}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{r}}}}\sim\tilde{\pi}_{\kappa_{r},t}}\tilde{r}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{r}}}})-\gamma^{t}\mathop{\mathbb{E}}\limits_{z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{r}}}}\sim\pi_{\kappa_{r},t}}\tilde{r}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{r}}}})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{t=\kappa_{c}-\kappa_{r}+1}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}\mathrm{TV}(\tilde{\pi}_{\kappa_{r},t},\pi_{\kappa_{r},t})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\gamma^{\kappa_{c}-\kappa_{r}+1}.

Take
\sup_{z}
on both sides above, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{z}\left\lvert\tilde{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i_{0}}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}})-C(z)\right\rvert\leq\frac{1}{1-\gamma}\gamma^{\kappa_{c}-\kappa_{r}+1}.

Appendix D
Proof of Theorem
5.3

In this section, we first derive the uniform properties of
\epsilon
-exploration policies, which are prerequisites for the critic error bound. Then we show that assumptions of Appendix
C
can be satisfied by Algorithm
2
under certain conditions. After that we restate Theorem
5.3
. Finally, we give the proof of the theorem, which is based on the results of localized stochastic approximation (Appendix
C
).

D.1  Uniformity of
\epsilon
-Exploration Policies

In order to derive a uniform critic error bound for all policies, we need uniform properties, , such as convergence rate, exploration, for the critic sampling policies, i.e.,
\epsilon
-exploration policy class
\Xi^{\epsilon}
.
This can be done by applying the results of
Zhang et al., 2023
when Assumption
5.3
holds.

Lemma D.1
.

For any policy
\hat{\xi}
from
\Xi^{\epsilon}
for
\epsilon>0
, the induced Markov chain
\{(s(t),a(t))\}
is aperiodic and irreducible.

Proof of Lemma
D.1
.

The result is obvious by point 1. of
(
Zhang et al., 2023
, Proposition 3)
and the fact that
\hat{\xi}(a|s)\geq\prod_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\frac{\epsilon}{\left\lvert\mathcal{A}_{i}\right\rvert}=\frac{\epsilon^{n}}{\left\lvert\mathcal{A}\right\rvert}>0
for any
s,a
.
∎

For any policy
\hat{\xi}\in\Xi^{\epsilon}
, we use the notation
\pi^{\hat{\xi}}_{t}(s,a)
to denote the probability of
(s,a)
at time
t
and use
\overline{\pi}^{\hat{\xi}}(s,a)
for the probability of
(s,a)
under stationary distribution. According to Lemma
D.1
, for any
\hat{\xi}\in\Xi^{\epsilon}
, there exists
c_{\hat{\xi}}>1
and
\rho_{\hat{\xi}}\in(0,1)
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}(\pi_{t}^{\hat{\xi}},\overline{\pi}^{\hat{\xi}})\leq c_{\hat{\xi}}\rho_{\hat{\xi}}^{t}.

(30)

Let
\overline{c}:=\sup_{\hat{\xi}\in\Xi^{\epsilon}}c_{\hat{\xi}}
,
\overline{\rho}:=\sup_{\hat{\xi}\in\Xi^{\epsilon}}\rho_{\hat{\xi}}
. Besides, recall the definitions of
\pi_{\min}
and
\underline{\lambda}
in Subsection
5.1
. The uniformity of
\Xi^{\epsilon}
can be shown by bounds of
\overline{\rho}
,
\pi_{\min}
and
\underline{\lambda}
.

Lemma D.2

(Uniformity of
\epsilon
-exploration policy class)
.

We have

1.

\overline{\rho}<1
. So
\pi_{t}^{\hat{\xi}}
has a uniform convergence rate for all
\hat{\xi}\in\Xi^{\epsilon}
.

2.

\pi_{\min}>0
. So each
(s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},a_{i})
pair is visited with positive probability, which has a uniform positive lower bound for all
\epsilon
-exploration polices.

3.

\underline{\lambda}>0
.

Proof of Lemma
D.2
.

Fix any
\hat{\xi}\in\Xi^{\epsilon}
. For part 1, by
(
Zhang et al., 2023
, Proposition 3)
, for any
\hat{\xi}\in\Xi^{\epsilon}
, there exists
\hat{c}>0
and
\rho_{0}<1
, such that
\sup_{\hat{\xi}\in\Xi^{\epsilon}}\max_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\mathrm{TV}(\pi_{\mathcal{S},t}^{\hat{\xi}}(\cdot|s),\overline{\pi}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\hat{\xi}})\leq\hat{c}\rho_{0}^{t}
for any
t\geq 0
. Here
\pi_{\mathcal{S},t}^{\hat{\xi}}(s^{\prime}|s)=\Pr_{\hat{\xi}}[s(t)=s^{\prime}|s(0)=s]
for any
s,s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S}
, and
\overline{\pi}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\hat{\xi}}\in\Delta(\mathcal{S})
is the stationary distribution of
\mathcal{S}
under policy
\hat{\xi}
. Then

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}(\pi_{t}^{\hat{\xi}},\overline{\pi}^{\hat{\xi}})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{s,a}\left\lvert\overline{\pi}_{t}^{\hat{\xi}}(s,a)-\overline{\pi}^{\hat{\xi}}(s,a)\right\rvert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{s,a}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}_{s^{0}\sim\mu}\left[\pi_{\mathcal{S},t}^{\hat{\xi}}(s|s^{0})\hat{\xi}(a|s)\right]-\overline{\pi}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\hat{\xi}}(s)\hat{\xi}(a|s)\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{s,a}\mathbb{E}_{s^{0}\sim\mu}\left[\left\lvert\pi_{\mathcal{S},t}^{\hat{\xi}}(s|s^{0})\hat{\xi}(a|s)-\overline{\pi}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\hat{\xi}}(s)\hat{\xi}(a|s)\right\rvert\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{s^{0}\sim\mu}\left[\sum_{s,a}\left\lvert\pi_{\mathcal{S},t}^{\hat{\xi}}(s|s^{0})-\overline{\pi}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\hat{\xi}}(s)\right\rvert\hat{\xi}(a|s)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{s^{0}\sim\mu}\left[\mathrm{TV}(\pi_{\mathcal{S},t}^{\hat{\xi}}(\cdot|s),\overline{\pi}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\hat{\xi}})\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\hat{c}\rho_{0}^{t}.

This shows that
\overline{\rho}\leq\rho_{0}<1
.

As for part 2, by 3. of
(
Zhang et al., 2023
, Proposition 3)
,
\underline{\pi}:=\inf_{\xi\in\Xi^{\epsilon}}\min_{s\in\mathcal{S}}\overline{\pi}_{\mathcal{S}}^{\hat{\xi}}(s)>0
. Thus for any
s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}\in\mathcal{S}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},a_{i}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
and any
\hat{\xi}\in\Xi^{\epsilon}
,

\displaystyle\overline{\pi}^{\hat{\xi}}(s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},a_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{s_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},a_{-i}}\overline{\pi}^{\hat{\xi}}(s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},s_{-{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},a_{i},a_{-i})

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\underline{\pi}

Thus
\pi_{\min}\geq\underline{\pi}>0
.

Finally we show part 3. For any square matrix
X
, denote by
\lambda_{\min}(X)
the minimum eigenvalue of
X
.
By definition of
\pi_{\min}
(see Subsection
5.1
), for any
\hat{\xi}\in\Xi^{\epsilon}
,
\lambda_{\min}(D^{\hat{\xi}})\geq\pi_{\min}
.

Since
\Omega_{i}
is full column rank,
\Omega_{i}^{\top}\Omega
is positive definite, so
\lambda_{\min}(\Omega_{i}^{\top}\Omega_{i})>0
for any
i\in\mathcal{N}
.

For any eigenvalue
\lambda
of
\Omega_{i}^{\top}D^{\hat{\xi}}\Omega_{i}
, let
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{i}}
be the corresponding eigenvector. Then

\displaystyle\lambda\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\lambda x^{\top}x

\displaystyle=x^{\top}\Omega_{i}^{\top}D^{\hat{\xi}}\Omega_{i}x

\displaystyle\geq\pi_{\min}\left\lVert\Omega_{i}x\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle=\pi_{\min}x^{T}\Omega_{i}^{\top}\Omega_{i}x

\displaystyle=\pi_{\min}\lambda_{\min}(\Omega_{i}^{\top}\Omega_{i})\left\lVert x\right\rVert^{2}.

Therefore
\lambda\geq\pi_{\min}\lambda_{\min}(\Omega_{i}^{\top}\Omega_{i})
, and thus
\underline{\lambda}_{i}^{\hat{\xi}}\geq\pi_{\min}\lambda_{\min}(\Omega_{i}^{\top}\Omega_{i})
. As a result,
\underline{\lambda}\geq\pi_{\min}\lambda_{\min}(\Omega_{i}^{\top}\Omega_{i})>0
.
∎

Besides the uniform convergence rate of the distribution
(s(t),a(t))
, we also want to establish the uniform convergence of the marginal distribution
(s(t),a_{i}(t))
for all
i\in\mathcal{N}
.

For any agent
i\in\mathcal{N}
and policy
\hat{\xi}\in\Xi^{\epsilon}
, we use the notation
\pi_{i,t}^{\hat{\xi}}(s,a_{i})
to denote the marginal probability of
(s,a_{i})
at time
t
and use
\overline{\pi}_{i}^{\hat{\xi}}(s,a_{i})
for the marginal probability of
(s,a_{i})
under stationary distribution. The following lemma shows that the distribution
(s(t),a_{i}(t))
converges as fast as the
(s,a)
, with a uniform convergence rate for all
\epsilon
-exploration policies.

Lemma D.3
.

The distribution of
(s(t),a_{i}(t))
has uniform convergence rate

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}(\pi^{\hat{\xi}}_{i,t},\overline{\pi}^{\hat{\xi}}_{i})\leq\overline{c}\cdot\overline{\rho}^{t}.

Here
\overline{c}
and
\overline{\rho}
are the uniform convergence rate of the distribution of
(s(t),a(t))
(see Appendix
D.1
).

Proof of Lemma
D.3
.

We have

\displaystyle\mathrm{TV}(\pi^{\hat{\xi}}_{i,t},\overline{\pi}^{\hat{\xi}}_{i})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{s,a_{i}}\left\lvert\pi^{\hat{\xi}}_{i,t}(s,a_{i})-\overline{\pi}^{\hat{\xi}}_{i}\right\rvert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{s,a_{i}}\left\lvert\sum_{a_{-i}}\pi^{\hat{\xi}}_{t}(s,a_{i},a_{-i})-\overline{\pi}^{\hat{\xi}}(s,a_{i},a_{-i})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\sum_{s,a_{i}}\sum_{a_{-i}}\left\lvert\pi^{\hat{\xi}}_{t}(s,a_{i},a_{-i})-\overline{\pi}^{\hat{\xi}}(s,a_{i},a_{-i})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\overline{c}\cdot\overline{\rho}^{t}.

Here the last inequality is due to Eq. (
30
), and
c_{\hat{\xi}}\leq\overline{c}
,
\rho_{\hat{\xi}}\leq\overline{\rho}
.
∎

D.2  Verifying assumptions of Appendix
C

In order to apply the results of localized stochastic approximation (Appendix
C
) , we verify the assumptions needed for Theorem
C.1
.
First, we correspond the problem setting of NMPG to the Markov chain setting of localized stochastic approximation.

We fix any policy
\hat{\xi}
, and any agent
i\in\mathcal{N}
for the rest of this section. Then
\{(s(t),a_{i}(t)\}
forms a Markov chain
\mathcal{M}^{i,\hat{\xi}}
.
Recall that
U_{j}^{\kappa}=N_{j}^{\kappa}/\{j\}
, which is the set of agents within
j
’s
\kappa
-hop neighborhood excluding
j
it self. Construct Markov chain
\mathcal{M}^{i,\hat{\xi}}=(\mathcal{N},\mathcal{E},\mathcal{Z},\mathbb{P},\tilde{r},\gamma,\mu^{\prime})
, and choose
i_{0}=i
. Here
\mathcal{N}
,
\mathcal{E}
and
\gamma
have the same meaning as in the NMPG setting (Section
2
), which are the set of agents, the edges of the graph and the discount factor, respectively. For the other elements, they are defined as

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{j}=\begin{cases}\mathcal{S}_{j}&j\neq i\\
\mathcal{S}_{j}\times\mathcal{A}_{j}&j=i\end{cases}

\displaystyle z_{j}=\begin{cases}s_{j}&j\neq i\\
(s_{j},a_{j})&j=i\end{cases}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{j}(z_{j}^{\prime}|z_{N_{j}})=\hat{\xi}_{j}(a_{j}^{\prime}|s_{j}^{\prime})\mathcal{P}_{j}(s_{j}^{\prime}|s_{N_{j}},a_{j}),\forall j\in\mathcal{N}

\displaystyle\mu^{\prime}(z)=\hat{\xi}(a|s)\mu(s)

\displaystyle\tilde{r}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{r}}}})=\overline{r}_{i}^{\hat{\xi}}(s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{r}}}},a_{i}):=\sum_{a_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{r}}}}}\hat{\xi}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{r}}}}(a_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{r}}}}|s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{r}}}})r_{i}(s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{r}}}},a_{i},a_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{r}}}}).

Here
\overline{r}_{i}^{\hat{\xi}}(s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{r}}}},a_{i})
is the expected reward function with respect to
a_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}U_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{r}}}}
. Furthermore, cost function
C(z)
of
\mathcal{M}^{i,\hat{\xi}}
corresponds to

\displaystyle C(s,a_{i})

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}{\mathbb{E}}_{\hat{\xi}}[\overline{r}_{i}^{\hat{\xi}}(s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{r}}}}(t),a_{i}(t))|s(0)=s,a_{i}(0)=a_{i}]

\displaystyle=\sum_{t=0}^{\infty}\gamma^{t}{\mathbb{E}}_{\hat{\xi}}[r_{i}(s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{r}}}}(t),a_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{r}}}}(t))|s(0)=s,a_{i}(0)=a_{i}]

\displaystyle=\overline{Q}_{i}^{\hat{\xi}}(s,a_{i}),

which is the averaged
Q
-function.

Now we represent localized TD(
\lambda
) with linear function approximation in the form of generalized TD(
\lambda
) (Algorithm
4
).
Let

\displaystyle d=d_{i},\ t_{0}=0

\displaystyle X(t)=(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(t),z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(t+1))

\displaystyle F(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(t),z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(t+1),w)=-\delta_{i}(t).

(31)

(Recall the definition of
\delta_{i}(t)
in Algorithm
2
.) Then Algorithm
4
analyzes the averaged
Q
-function of agent
i
.

Next we show that Assumptions
C.1
,
C.2
,
C.3
are satisfied by localized TD(
\lambda
) under the conditions of Theorem
5.3
.

Verify Assumption
C.1
.

This can be shown by the following lemma.

Lemma D.4
.

For any policy
\hat{\xi}\in\Xi^{\epsilon}
,
\epsilon>0
,
i\in\mathcal{N}
, the induced Markov chain
\{(s(t),a_{i}(t))\}
is aperiodic and irreducible.

Proof of Lemma
D.4
.

For any Markov chain with transition probability
\Gamma
on some state space
\mathcal{X}
, we write
\Gamma^{l}(x^{\prime}|x)=\Pr[x(t+l)=x^{\prime}|x(t)=x]
, for any
t\in\mathbb{N}
,
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{X}
.

We fix
i\in\mathcal{N}
. By Lemma
D.2
, the induced Markov chain
\{(s(t),a(t))\}
is aperiodic and irreducible. We abuse the notation in this proof to use
\mathbb{P}
for the transition probability of
(s,a_{i})

and use
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
for that of
(s,a)
.

For any

s,s^{\prime}\in\mathcal{S},a_{i},a_{i}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{i}
, we randomly pick
a_{-i},a_{-i}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{-i}
.
Since
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
is irreducible, there exists
t>0
, such that

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}^{t}(s^{\prime},a_{i}^{\prime},a_{-i}^{\prime}|s,a_{i},a_{-i})>0
. We choose the smallest
t_{0}>0
such that
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}^{t_{0}}(s^{\prime},a_{i}^{\prime},a_{-i}^{\prime}|s,a_{i},a_{-i})>0
for any
a_{-i},a_{-i}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}_{-i}
,
so
\mathbb{P}^{t}_{i}(s^{\prime},a_{i}^{\prime}|s,a_{i},a_{-i})>0
.
\mathbb{P}^{t}_{i}(s^{\prime},a_{i}^{\prime}|s,a_{i},a_{-i})
represents the marginal probability of
s^{\prime},a_{i}^{\prime}
given previous state-action pair
(s,a_{i},a_{-i})
. Then we have

\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}^{t_{0}}(s^{\prime},a_{i}^{\prime}|s,a_{i})

\displaystyle=\sum_{\hat{a}_{-i}\in\mathcal{A}_{-i}}\hat{\xi}_{-i}(\hat{a}_{-i}|s_{-i})\mathbb{P}^{t_{0}}_{i}(s^{\prime},a_{i}^{\prime}|s,a_{i},\hat{a}_{-i})

\displaystyle\geq\min_{\hat{a}_{-i}\in\mathcal{A}_{-i}}\mathbb{P}^{t_{0}}_{i}(s^{\prime},a_{i}^{\prime}|s,a_{i},\hat{a}_{-i})

\displaystyle>0.

Therefore,
\mathbb{P}
is irreducible.

To show that
\mathbb{P}
is aperiodic, we assume that
\mathbb{P}
has period
T\geq 2
. Then for any

t
not divisible by
T
and any
s,a_{i}
,
\mathbb{P}^{t}(s,a_{i}|s,a_{i})=0
. For any

s,a
, since
t
not divisible by
T
, we have

\displaystyle 0=\mathbb{P}^{t}(s,a_{i}|s,a_{i})=\sum_{\hat{a}_{-i}\in\mathcal{A}_{-i}}\hat{\xi}_{-i}(\hat{a}_{-i}|s_{-i})\mathbb{P}^{t}_{i}(s,a_{i}|s,a_{i},\hat{a}_{-i}).

Thus we have
\mathbb{P}^{t}_{i}(s,a_{i}|s,a_{i},\hat{a}_{-i})=0
for some

\hat{a}_{-i}
and thus
\mathbb{P}^{t}_{i}(s,a_{i},\hat{a}_{-i}|s,a_{i},\hat{a}_{-i})=0
. This implies that the
period of
(s,a_{i},\hat{a}_{-i})
is at least
T\geq 2
, which contradicts the assumption that
\mathbb{P}
is aperiodic. (Notice that the period of any state is the same for an irreducible Markov chain.) Hence

{\mathbb{P}}
is aperiodic.

In conclusion,
{\mathbb{P}}
is irreducible and aperiodic.
∎

Verify Assumption
C.2
.

We show that for any agent
i\in\mathcal{N}
and any policy
\hat{\xi}\in\Xi^{\epsilon}
, the assumption can be satisfied for
L_{1}=1+\gamma
and
c_{0}=(1-\gamma)\underline{\lambda}
.

We first verify 1. of Assumption
C.2
. For any
w_{1},w_{2},x
, we have

\displaystyle\left\lVert F(x,w_{1})-F(x,w_{2})\right\rVert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\lVert\langle\phi_{i}(s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(t),a_{i}(t)),w_{1}-w_{2}\rangle-\gamma\langle\phi_{i}(s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(t+1),a_{i}(t+1)),w_{1}-w_{2}\rangle\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\lVert\langle\phi_{i}(s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(t),a_{i}(t)),w_{1}-w_{2}\rangle\right\rVert+\gamma\left\lVert\langle\phi_{i}(s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}(t+1),a_{i}(t+1)),w_{1}-w_{2}\rangle\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1+\gamma)\|w_{1}-w_{2}\|.

For any
x
, notice that

\displaystyle\|F(x,0)\|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\lVert\overline{r}_{i}^{\hat{\xi}}(s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{r}}}}(t),a_{i}(t))\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 1

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 1+\gamma.

Now we prove 2. in Assumption
C.2
.
We point out that our localized TD(
\lambda
) can be easily reduced to the classical single-agent TD(
\lambda
) algorithm, with
\mathcal{Z}
being the single-agent state space.
Then by
Tsitsiklis and Van Roy, 1997
,
\overline{G}(w)
has a unique zero
w^{*}
. Furthermore, then according to the proof of Lemma 9 in
Tsitsiklis and Van Roy, 1997
, we have

\displaystyle\langle w-w^{*},\overline{G}(w)\rangle

\displaystyle\leq-(1-\gamma)\left\lVert\Omega_{i}w-\Omega_{i}w^{*}\right\rVert_{D^{\hat{\xi}}}

\displaystyle=-(1-\gamma)(w-w^{*})^{\top}\Omega_{i}^{\top}D^{\hat{\xi}}\Omega_{i}(w-w^{*})

\displaystyle\leq-(1-\gamma)\underline{\lambda}_{k}^{\hat{\xi}}\left\lVert w-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}

\displaystyle\leq-(1-\gamma)\underline{\lambda}\left\lVert w-w^{*}\right\rVert^{2}.

We will sometimes add a subscript
i
for
w^{*}
and write
w_{i}^{*}
to distinguish different agents.

Verify Assumption
C.3
.

Approximate cost function
\hat{C}
in the localized stochastic approximation problem corresponds to the approximate averaged
Q
-function of agent
i
, i.e.,
\hat{Q}_{i}(s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},a_{i},w_{i})=\langle\phi_{i}(s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},a_{i}),w_{i}\rangle
. Since
\left\lVert\phi_{i}(s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},a_{i})\right\rVert\leq 1
,
we have for any
w_{1},w_{2}

\displaystyle\left\lvert\hat{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},w_{1})-\hat{C}(z_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},w_{2})\right\rvert

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\lvert\langle\phi_{i}(s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},a_{i}),w_{1}-w_{2}\rangle\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\lVert\phi_{i}(s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},a_{i})\right\rVert\left\lVert w_{1}-w_{2}\right\rVert

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\lVert w_{1}-w_{2}\right\rVert.

So Assumption
C.3
can be satisfied with
L_{2}=1
.

D.3  Restatement of Theorem
5.3

Before giving the precise form of Theorem
5.3
,
We define two quantities needed for the theorem statement. The first quantity is the function approximation error.
Denote by
\mathcal{M}_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}}^{\hat{\xi}}
the sub-chain of
\mathcal{M}^{i,\hat{\xi}}
(defined in Appendix
D.2
) with respect to agents in
{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}
, and let
\tilde{Q}_{i}^{\hat{\xi}}(s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},a_{i})
be the cost function of
\mathcal{M}^{i,\hat{\xi}}

at state
(s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},a_{i})
.

Definition D.1
.

For any policy
\hat{\xi}\in\Xi^{\epsilon}
, and any agent
i\in\mathcal{N}
, define the function approximation error as

\displaystyle\epsilon_{app}:=\sup_{\hat{\xi}\in\Xi^{\epsilon}}\sup_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\inf_{w}\sup_{s,a_{i}}\left\lvert\hat{Q}_{i}(s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},a_{i},w)-\tilde{Q}_{i}^{\hat{\xi}}(s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},a_{i})\right\rvert.

Notice that we measure the representation power of the function approximation with respect to the sub-chain instead of the original MDP.

The second quantity we need is the uniform mixing time of the update term
-\delta_{i}(t)\zeta_{i}^{\kappa_{c}}(t)
in Algorithm
2
, corresponding to function
G
in Appendix
C
.
Notice update term
-\delta_{i}(t)\zeta_{i}^{\kappa_{c}}(t)
does not depend on policy
\hat{\xi}
, and there is a uniform decay rate of
(s(t),a_{i}(t)
for any
i
and any policy
\hat{\xi}\in\Xi^{\epsilon}
(Lemma
D.3
). Then we can apply Lemma
C.9
for all policy
\hat{\xi}\in\Xi^{\epsilon}
and get a uniform decay rate
c_{\text{TD}}:=c_{g}(\overline{c},\overline{\rho},\lambda,0)
,
\rho_{\text{TD}}:=\rho_{g}(\overline{\rho},\lambda)
. This indicates that there is a uniform “mixing time of function
G
with precision
\delta
” (Definition
C.1
) for all policies
\hat{\xi}\in\Xi^{\epsilon}
and all
\delta>0
, which we denote by
t_{\delta}
. In addition,
t_{\delta}=O(\log\frac{1}{\delta})
.

We now give a stronger version of Theorem
5.3
. We can derive Theorem
5.3
by taking square root on both sides and apply Jenson’s inequality on the right hand side.

Theorem D.1

(Restatement of Theorem
5.3
)
.

Recall the definition of uniform mixing time
t_{\alpha}
above.
Choose step size
\alpha
such that
\alpha t_{\alpha}\leq\min\left\{\frac{1-\lambda}{4(1+\gamma)},\frac{(1-\gamma)(1-\lambda)^{2}\underline{\lambda}}{114(1+\gamma)^{2}}\right\}
.
Then we have for any
K\geq t_{\alpha}
and any
i\in\mathcal{N}

\displaystyle\epsilon_{critic}^{2}=

\displaystyle\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\sup_{\theta}\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{s,a_{i}}\left\lvert\hat{Q}_{i}(s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},a_{i},w_{i}(K))-\overline{Q}_{i}^{\theta}(s,a_{i})\right\rvert^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\left[c_{1}^{*}(1-(1-\gamma)\underline{\lambda}\alpha)^{K-t_{\alpha}}+c_{2}^{*}\frac{\alpha t_{\alpha}}{(1-\gamma)\underline{\lambda}}+\left(\frac{(1-\lambda\gamma)\epsilon_{app}}{\pi_{\min}(1-\gamma)}\right)^{2}\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\left(\frac{\gamma^{\kappa_{c}-\kappa_{r}+1}}{1-\gamma}\right)^{2}+\left(\frac{6n\epsilon}{(1-\gamma)^{2}}\right)^{2}\right].

Here
c_{1}^{*}=(\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\|w_{i}^{*}\|+1)^{2}
,
c_{2}^{*}=114\left(\frac{1+\gamma}{1-\lambda}\right)^{2}(\max_{i\in\mathcal{N}}\left\lVert w_{i}^{*}\right\rVert+1)^{2}
.

D.4  Proof of Theorem
D.1

Throughout the proof, we will fix agent
i\in\mathcal{N}
and policy
\xi^{\theta}
.
For any policy parameter
\theta
, let
\hat{\xi}
be the
\epsilon
-exploration policy of
\xi^{\theta}
. We can decompose the critic error as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{s,a_{i}}\left\lvert\hat{Q}_{i}(s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},a_{i},w_{i}(K))-\overline{Q}_{i}^{\theta}(s,a_{i})\right\rvert^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(i)}{\leq}

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{s,a_{i}}(3+1)\left(\frac{1}{3}\left\lvert\hat{Q}_{i}(s_{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}N_{i}^{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\kappa}_{c}}}},a_{i},w_{i}(K))-\overline{Q}_{i}^{\hat{\xi}}(s,a_{i})\right\rvert^{2}+\left\lvert\overline{Q}_{i}^{\hat{\xi}}(s,a_{i})-\overline{Q}_{i}^{\theta}(s,a_{i})\right\rvert^{2}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\overset{(ii)}{\leq}
